Exact rollback restores both handles, historical header and the plan acknowledged by actual committed scan dispatch. This is native history/two-clock compatibility, not combined deep-clock/index/population/reference cost, full host upper, fee backing or Executive chronology.

The shared temporal/history source now feeds `close_public_wide_idle a30..38`: last31-hole target Pending, interior63-hole cursor32/no target event, then cursor33/target event, each User peer/User last-slot/System. `prepare_temporal_wide_history_close` owns the source and actual singleton scan unit; `assert_wide_idle_close_checks` owns common cleanup and independent Tick retirement. Native rollback snapshots follow the entire actual source prefix. Verification enums carry benchmark-only data, not consensus storage. Setup/checks stay outside the unchanged complete public Close block; a0..29/late-hold0..1 keep their source semantics. The DEOS reference's finite temporal/history whole-call observations now belong to the root Actors integration evidence map; they do not qualify combined deep-clock/population or complete caller bounds. Production code, production artifacts and numerical prices remain unchanged.

`prepare_temporal_wide_history_close_with_delay` selects an admissible horizon before public creation and actual peer history; the existing wrapper keeps its maximum-horizon default and all profile mappings. `temporal_wide_close_repairs_both_deep_indices_with_history_and_rollback` chooses headroom for distinct future Tick guards and joins interior63-hole/cursor33/Pending to two multi-page clock indices. Ordinary Cadenced and Manual/future-window System guards share remaining effective active, identity and allocated-System-slot capacity. Actual target positions are observed, not forced to roots. Close retires both unique keys/tail pages, preserves64 source peers and every guard identity/semantic/process/custody/bucket snapshot; exact caller rollback restores the post-setup root. Source guards, peers and authenticated Contract stay intact.

Post-close counter expectations are captured after guard creation; this changes observer data, never committed progress. `SystemSovereignCount` remains allocated `SystemSovereigns` cardinality, including Vacant slots, so System Close preserves it. The root integration owns finite native population/index coordinates and reference applicability; this join proves no configured/global upper, full Executive chronology, economic backing or new-domain reference cost.

`prepare_wide_idle_deep_temporal_close` now owns the shared deep source; `WideIdleDeepClockChecks` carries benchmark-only budget, two key/length pairs, allocated System-slot count and guard snapshots. `wide_idle_deep_clock_guard_snapshot` SCALE-encodes the same identity/parked-state/key/bucket-header/page fields formerly compared as native tuples, with no consensus storage or projection. `assert_wide_idle_deep_clock_close` owns key/tail/slot/guard checks alongside shared interior verification. Existing `close_public_wide_idle a39..41` selects the three deep role/slot cases, while a0..38 and late-hold0..1 keep their mappings. Native outer rollback and SDK verification on/off use the same preparation/checks; all setup/snapshots/checks remain outside the unchanged complete public Close block.

The root integration owns the source-bound finite reference collection now covering this shared deep join and the unresolved complete upper/backing gates. Native index positions and effective admission limits are not reference coordinates; only executed reference guards and their exported observations support that host domain.

`prepare_wide_idle_active_interior_scan` accepts an already authenticated historical source, while `prepare_wide_idle_deep_temporal_close_from_source` adds ordinary guards to prepared temporal/interior checks. Existing wrappers preserve their original flow; these boundaries allow a legal review prefix before causal publication rather than reconfiguring an already scanned source. `assert_wide_idle_deep_temporal_caller` is test-only and owns complete Close/caller rollback for both native interior-index and both-root witnesses.

`temporal_wide_close_rearms_earlier_reviews_before_deep_root_cleanup` advances monotone prepared successor blocks and runs one `service_due_deadline_frontiers` quantum per block with actual host tick and `Block(now+1)`, matching production Prepass arguments. Each earlier source peer receives a negative same-generation rearm at most once; the bounded loop stops before selecting the target. Its target parked/custody/plan snapshot and full source header remain exact, with no scan active. Only afterward does ordinary publication/scan create cursor33/Pending and deep guards. Both target keys become minima through real review transitions; no heap position, clock carrier, cursor or process authority is rewritten. Caller rollback preserves all committed preparation. Native worker compatibility does not claim full Executive chronology, current-block capacity, reference cost or a configured/global upper.

`prepare_wide_idle_root_review_prefix<T>` now owns the bounded earlier-peer worker prefix, additionally authenticating the complete SCALE Contract across it. `prepare_wide_idle_root_temporal_close<T>` composes historical source, review prefix, real scan, deep guards and both-minimum checks for native caller tests and `close_public_wide_idle a42..44`; the old a0..41/late0..1 mappings remain unchanged. Test-only prefix diagnostics export no consensus storage or runtime API. SDK verification on/off and native complete caller rollback use identical preparation and cleanup; review work, captures and checks stay outside the unchanged complete public Close block. Reference applicability and all complete upper/backing claims remain in the root integration evidence owner.

Both-minimum checks are required benchmark source-admission predicates, not promises that every host satisfies the workload. A native/SDK success cannot replace an actual reference-host predicate failure; a setup trap before the recorded Close produces no accepted measurement. The root integration owns failed-artifact identity and concrete host diagnosis, while the package keeps its generic source checks fail-closed.

The root-only source authors finite80-tick AtTime before creation/history; other temporal sources retain their inputs. Natural reactivation keeps that field but anchors the new generation's clock. `prepare_wide_idle_root_review_prefix<T>` preserves the peer bound, monotone blocks, actual tick and B+1, refusing before any due target mutation. A confirmed singleton unanchored Idle/noSignal/noRun Tick0 may anchor only in the first existing paired Block-then-Tick Live quantum; the returned tick records that exact transition. No extra pass or Actor-id policy exists, and further due Tick work is refused before another quantum. The chosen root must be unique and future after owed startup, never by direct carrier mutation.

`wide_idle_tick_peer_authority<T>` snapshots the singleton minimum other Tick peer from the root's two children. `WideIdleDeepClockChecks::retained_tick` preserves identity/generation/semantic/process/full Contract/handle/bucket/page across preparation and Close. For genuine genesis, that future-peer snapshot follows the committed bootstrap/review prefix; for already-anchored peers, the previous pre-prefix snapshot remains. Custody always follows all committed prefixes, and ingress is not rewound. Non-root deep checks keep the owner absent. Tests use real pallet genesis through a scoped test-host provider, preserving defaultempty storage builds; no fixture injects an anchor/carrier. They cover normal callers, SDK±, earlier/shared/expiry refusal, multiple bootstrap members and a second due occurrence.

`observe_wide_idle_deadline_minima<T>` emits clock-tagged, bounded source diagnostics at initial admission refusal and after review prefix, publication/scan and each guard family. It reads two index root pages, at most one C32 member page per clock and the first member's identity/process/full Contract/temporal anchor/due state; target refs, handles, inverse positions and bucket counts disambiguate predicate failure. All queries/logging stay outside recorded Close, with no consensus state, hook, counter or public fixture API. Root assertions and source transitions remain unchanged. Concrete startup ownership and host applicability belong to the root integration.

`prepare_prefix_close_with_key` additionally stages the wide no-policy Suspended Actor for singleton last-key removal. It preserves the authored Steps, public creation, matched ingress and post-execution width/custody guards. Its configured cooldown and extended ScheduleWindow contain the future retry. For the last User slot, ordinary Dormant peers retire before allocating the guard population, so capacity follows the surviving identity state. Other constructor selections preserve the original cooldown/window and two Service opportunities.

`prepare_close_deadline_key_remove_with_authority` shares the exact preceding insertion and independently simulated repair algorithm with the pure-key constructor. Guard authority comes from public System creation/readiness and canonical future-window residence transfer. The target's actual second failed Step publishes its retry at the chosen insertion point; no target Deadline/Run is fabricated. Root, widest root descent, interior descent and upward repair retain singleton buckets, the configured heap depth/page-address checks, exact expected index/reverse positions and every surviving peer handle. Tail occupancy one reclaims the final index page; occupancy thirty-two leaves thirty-one entries. Every measured Block key remains future-dated. This is a prepared canonical-state construction, not a complete historical public setup replay.

`close_public_wide_deadline_key(a=0..23)` selects those four geometries in groups of six: User peer-slot, User last-slot and System, each with tail one/thirty-two. Its late-hold companion selects the sixteen User geometry/slot/tail combinations. Measurement includes the complete signed-owner Pallet Close; setup, peer retirement, capture/verification and failure repair/successful Close are excluded. Shared neighbor and destruction checks validate complete cleanup; exact-root currency rollback preserves the complete Actor, heap, custody and prefix before repair. Two native tests pass fifty-seven scenes; eight affected prior tests and four reference-Wasm profiles retain their declared scopes in the integration owner. These joins do not certify other clock/residence branches, Parked population/history, optional/financial companions, complete event/currency/host input domains, SignedExtra/Executive, physical upper bounds or tariffs.

Reference resource derivation uses canonical Step context, non-Stop allowances and the fixed Parked completion owner, without Actor-class or recipient-fanout dimensions. `admitted_no_policy_resource_codec_stays_within_bound` compares equivalent retained Task operands and ContinueNextStep. Task/watch/Run changes require fresh physical and resource binding; prior codec/port observations do not certify this tree.

The [reference integration](../../../../docs/deos/actors.integration.en.md#actor-state-holds-and-capacity) owns numeric header, body-prefix and joint value totals, exact source/artifact bindings and exclusions. The sum covers unique Contract head/tails plus one Run value; it does not bound other semantic/process/certificate/companion values, trie proofs, repeated reconstruction/hash work, FRAME/currency/event/transaction host costs or RefTime. Generic 36-byte resource metadata and state-hold capacity remain unchanged. A reserve, generated Weight, adapter, admission or body/Run/header codec change requires requalification; no physical coefficient or tariff follows from this join.

The extra `close_deadline_retry_authority` isolates Block removal after two real temporary Action failures. `assert_deadline_process_authority_width` verifies the branch-legal maximum for `Serving` plus `Some(Deadline)` with populated `last_attempted`, rather than the larger global `ActorProcess::max_encoded_len()`. The verifier preserves the suspended Run, process/semantic authority, independent Tick Trigger, holds and peer handles; cancellation and complete Actor removal occur only afterward, outside measurement. `pure_deadline_process_authority_width_requires_attempt_history` exercises both verification modes, and the profile runs in benchmark Wasm. This record-width witness does not by itself combine width with every page/index extremum or establish a complete cleanup bound.

The extra `close_deadline_attempted_authority` combines that Process width with the nine-page unlink geometry and four full index-page reads. `prepare_retry_authority` installs a two-Step Manual Contract: Opening skips a false predicate, then a real temporary Transfer failure in the retained Run publishes a long-cooldown Block retry. Before that attempt, 96 peers occupy the first three pages; the target joins page 3, slot 0. `prepare_close_authority_page_prefix` and `finish_close_authority_page` share the remaining setup with the completed-Trigger fixture. `pure_deadline_attempted_authority_preserves_width_and_geometry` verifies exact neighbors/peers and both verification modes, retaining the suspended Run until unmeasured teardown. Both joint profiles run in benchmark Wasm; last-key repair and complete Close remain separate cost domains.

The extra `close_trigger_completed_authority` measures pure Tick removal for a System Actor after an ordinary Cadenced cycle. Its identity reaches the host type's maximum width, and its semantic record contains the temporal anchor, independent Trigger pointer and completed-cycle block. `assert_completed_temporal_authority_width` excludes canonical legacy-placement fields and `terminal_at`: temporal Triggers reject `ScheduleWindow`. The compact lifecycle Weight uses the current host admission certificate, not fabricated `Weight::MAX`.

Ordinary pause/resume retains completed-cycle history while 96 peers take the first three pages; resume installs the target at page 3, slot 0. Further admission and peer closes produce nine pages with the same four full-width unlink neighbors as the page fixture. Index position 47 supplies four full index-page reads. `capture_close_deadline_page_geometry` shares exact neighbor, header and 252-peer postconditions with the existing page profiles. This is a joint unlink witness, not a bound for every last-key repair or complete Close.

`pure_trigger_authority_width_covers_legal_temporal_shapes` rejects windows for both temporal families, checks the completed Cadenced width, and proves that an unconsumed AtTime source has no completed-cycle block; its extra consumed flag does not dominate that Cadenced record. Both verification modes preserve unmeasured authority, and the profile runs in benchmark Wasm. Shared `finish_close_after_deadline_removal` clears consumed pointers and performs remaining residence/lifecycle teardown only after the pure-removal checks; the existing page regressions cover that shared verifier.

The paired `close_deadline_page_retain` and `close_trigger_page_retain` extras reuse the attempted-Process and completed-Trigger constructors, placing the wide authority at page 1, slot 0 after 32 ordinary peers. Five pages supply full first/last boundaries, a full interior target and a distinct vacancy head with a successor; four full index pages retain position 47. Pure removal retains the target page and prepends it to the vacancy list without changing physical links. The shared verifier preserves every peer handle, process/semantic/Service authority and custody. `pure_deadline_page_retention_preserves_vacancies_and_close_boundary` also checks both wide authorities in an already-vacant, non-head page. Both full-page profiles run in benchmark Wasm; verification-disabled tests retain the suspended Run and completed temporal record until unmeasured teardown.

The common page constructor leaves every indexed key of the measured clock strictly in the future at the measurement boundary. Prefix keys reserve time for admission, pause/resume and peer closes; an explicit assertion rejects geometry that depends on skipping an already-due frontier. The joint unlink and retained-page profiles therefore share both the record-width checks and this temporal setup guard. These witnesses do not establish worst-case trie proof, interior last-key repair or complete Close bounds.

`assert_close_deadline_page_width` checks all 32 retained slots, live occupancy, link presence and exact SCALE width; `capture_close_deadline_fixture` also requires the maximum encoded `DeadlineHeader`. With `M = DeadlinePage::max_encoded_len()`, `R = ActorRef::max_encoded_len()` and `U = u64::max_encoded_len()`, the prepared roles have these pre-removal widths:

| Page role | Live slots | Present physical/vacancy links | Encoded width |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Last member of a key | 1 | 0 / 0 | `M - 31R - 4U` |
| Unlinked interior target | 1 | 2 / 2 | `M - 31R` |
| Full interior target or physical neighbor | 32 | 2 / 0 | `M - 2U` |
| Interior vacancy neighbor | 31 | 2 / 2 | `M - R` |
| Previous vacancy head during full-page retention | 31 | 2 / 1 | `M - R - U` |
| Full first/last boundary | 32 | 1 / 0 | `M - 3U` |

Full pages cannot carry vacancy links; a last-member key has no page neighbors. A full physical page and a 31-member page with both vacancy links tie in width for the current Actor reference encoding; the fixture checks the larger legal expression rather than assuming that full occupancy alone maximizes bytes. Singleton pages already allocate all 32 slots, so artificial fill/close churn is unnecessary. These checks qualify page/header payloads, not process or semantic authority records or complete Weight.

The shared fixture fills a 128-key clock index through ordinary Actor creation, retaining host genesis. Increasing keys place the measured source at index 47; its parent 23 and children 95/96 occupy three other full pages. This exposes all four distinct index-page reads in `update_deadline_index` without fabricating heap entries. Exact pre/post index snapshots plus the bijection check prove surviving-key removal leaves the index unchanged. Fresh benchmark-Wasm runs cover all four profiles; their database reports distinguish member-page access from index-page validation.

The last-member key-removal profiles stage the same legal-width authorities as the page profiles before populating the heap. `prepare_retry_authority` retains a Running Contract immediately before its real temporary failure; completed Cadenced authority is paused, then resumed at the selected insertion point. `publish_close_deadline_authority` asserts the resulting width and exact Deadline key. Upward/interior priorities are translated around that existing future key, not installed by editing handles or history. The staged Actor already consumes one identity/slot, so capacity accounting adds only its unpublished key. Guard spacing obeys ordinary admission limits, clocks never rewind, and every measured-clock key remains future-dated. The verifier compares the retained Run bytes before unmeasured cancellation/finalization.

`remove_deadline_index` has a source-level work bound independent of measured heap shape. Let `N = MaxActiveActors` and `H = bit_length(max(N, 1))`, matching `deadline_index_height_bound`. Outside the repair loop there are at most ten explicit storage reads and five writes. Each of at most `H` iterations performs at most twelve reads and four writes: the downward case includes parent/current checks, child selection and `deadline_index_swap`; upward repair is cheaper. Early returns and failed lookups only shorten this access sequence. Thus explicit calls are bounded by `10 + 12H` reads and `5 + 4H` writes, including repeated keys but excluding enclosing member removal, transaction machinery and the rest of Close. These are operation counts, not DB Weight or RefTime coefficients.

| Index-removal owner | Read calls | Write calls | Distinct-key bound |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `DeadlineIndexPages` | `4 + 10H` | `2 + 2H` | At most `min(ceil(N/32), 3H+2)` read pages and `min(ceil(N/32), H+2)` written pages |
| `DeadlineIndexPositions` | `2 + 2H` | `2 + 2H` | At most `H+2` keys: removed key, relocating tail and one new key per swap |
| `DeadlineIndexLen` | `1` | `1` | One clock-local key |
| Header absence and two legacy guards | `3` | `0` | Three fixed keys |

At `N=10,000`, `H=14`: at most 178 read calls, 61 write calls and 144 index-page decodes, each bounded to 32 entries. For valid heap topology, the existing address model tightens the distinct-page bounds to 18 reads and 11 writes; ascending repair touches a subset of the parent/child neighborhood of the same root-to-node path, including the stopping node's child check. Together with the reverse-position and fixed-row bounds, index removal alone therefore needs at most 38 distinct read keys and 28 distinct written keys. The counts do not include Process/Trigger authority, bucket members or enclosing Close work.

The [Close proof-input model](#close-cost-partition) now composes this distinct-key inventory with native key/value ceilings, declared namespace and foreign-key contracts, and justified bucket/clock prefix sharing. It covers member authority and page removal alongside index repair; raw record bytes or the largest observed proof are not substituted for a bound. RefTime qualification still must cover repeated page decoding/comparison, reverse-position validation and rollback. Existing joint authority/index profiles are calibration witnesses for those obligations, not automatic coefficients. Additional sharing may reduce an observation but cannot be deducted without a separate proof.

`close_deadline_key_remove(n)` and `close_trigger_key_remove(n)` isolate deletion of a bucket's last member and minimum heap key. Ordinary creation holds a full index prefix fixed near host capacity while varying tail occupancy over `1..=32`. Inserting the minimum at slot 31 establishes a leftmost sink path; the replacement reaches the deepest level allowed by the active cap. Exact heap pages, reverse-index checks and peer handles verify repair: one tail entry reclaims its page, while larger tails retain `n - 1` entries. Earlier host Tick occurrences settle through the ordinary dispatcher before population. `pure_deadline_key_removal_covers_tail_page_outcomes` covers both owners, ordinary host progress without genesis replacement and verification-disabled isolation; fresh benchmark-Wasm endpoint runs cover both tail outcomes without treating fitted diagnostic coefficients as a production maximum.

`close_deadline_key_remove_wide(n)` and `close_trigger_key_remove_wide(n)` enumerate stable prefix leaves, maximizing distinct written index pages, then read pages and path length. `close_deadline_descent_pages` models the parent, child, replacement and tail page addresses in `remove_deadline_index`, not timing or trie-proof bytes. Setup selects a minimum-insertion point on the chosen path, inverts its ancestor shift and creates every key through ordinary admission while retaining host genesis. Ranking and prefix stay fixed across tail occupancy. Exact swap/changed-page checks, `pure_deadline_key_removal_wide_path_preserves_peers_and_isolation` and fresh benchmark-Wasm endpoint runs cover both owners and tail outcomes. The selector does not claim a universal Weight maximum.

`pure_deadline_descent_geometry_covers_interior_suffixes` enumerates every root stopping point and interior suffix in full trees capped at 1024 and 10000 keys. The model places the popped tail on a distinct abstract page, never in storage or a measured fixture. Smaller heaps only prune child reads from these paths; a real tail can add at most that one page, and suffix address sets stay within their root extension. Against `remove_deadline_index` and its get/set/swap helpers, this bounds unique index-page reads/writes by 11/7 and 18/11 respectively for any population up to those caps. The actual leftmost and selected-wide witnesses jointly attain both coordinates and keep their geometry across all 32 tail occupancies.

These are index-page address bounds, not total database-operation, RefTime, trie-proof or encoded-authority bounds. The two coordinates need not come from one witness. Executable fixtures still pass the actual tail page, so this population-proof generalization changes neither their selected paths nor their measured bodies; existing benchmark-Wasm structural observations remain applicable. Other capacity limits require their own bound check, and the complete cleanup owner remains unqualified.

`close_deadline_key_remove_interior(n)` and `close_trigger_key_remove_interior(n)` select a stable non-root descent after the host-owned prefix, using the same written-page/read-page/path-length ordering as the root selector. Ranking the selected path and its ancestors below siblings permits ordinary insertion without fabricated heap entries. Exact swaps, changed pages, reverse positions, peer handles and custody are checked outside measurement. Native tests cover both owners, both tail-page outcomes and verification-disabled isolation; the address model checks all 32 tail occupancies. Both profiles run in benchmark Wasm alongside the root-wide and upward profiles on one artifact. The staged authority supplies the qualified record width, but these interior observations do not certify RefTime/trie-proof dominance.

`close_deadline_key_remove_upward(n)` and `close_trigger_key_remove_upward(n)` cover non-root removal with upward replacement. A fixed blueprint ranks all possible tail entries and their ancestors below other keys, preserving heap order during ordinary Actor creation. A bounded search selects the off-spine leaf with the most distinct write pages within that blueprint, breaking ties by ascent length. Both tail endpoints retain the same prefix and ascent path. Exact expected heap/changed-page counts, peer handles and `pure_deadline_key_removal_repairs_upward_and_preserves_peers` verify repair and isolation. Fresh benchmark-Wasm endpoint runs cover both owners; a larger index-page write footprint need not imply more total database writes.

`pure_deadline_interior_descent_crosses_more_write_pages_than_leftmost_path` constructs 993 or 1024 keys through ordinary creation, ranking one ancestor-closed path below its siblings. Removal at index 1 descends through right child 32 and then left children, changing seven index pages including the tail, versus six for the leftmost root path at the same population. Both process and Trigger cases preserve exact remaining pages, reverse positions, peer handles and custody. This native counterexample shows that maximum depth alone does not maximize page writes; it establishes neither a RefTime comparison nor a production ProofSize bound. Address-set inclusion under root extension does not by itself certify RefTime or trie-proof dominance.

`pure_deadline_key_repair_late_failure_rolls_back` corrupts the reverse position needed by the second swap for root-wide descent, interior descent and upward repair, on both clocks. The failed transaction restores the exact pre-call storage root despite prior tail-page reclamation and the first swap. After restoring the corrupted position, the same removal succeeds and satisfies the full heap/peer/custody verifier. This proves failed-call rollback, not compensation of previously committed Actor Steps.

Direct-tail index removal takes the common prefix of `lib.rs::remove_deadline_index`: validate the target/tail positions, pop or delete the tail page, remove the target reverse position and decrement the length. `index == last` then returns before replacement and heap repair. The root-removal profiles already contain this prefix and both tail-page outcomes; direct-tail aliases target and tail accesses rather than adding an index owner. `pure_deadline_tail_removal_preserves_the_index_prefix` verifies both process and Trigger removal with 1, 32 and 33 keys, covering an empty heap, retained tail and reclaimed singleton tail. Exact snapshots, reverse-index checks and peer handles preserve every surviving key. This is structural branch coverage, not a new RefTime measurement or a certified production coefficient.

`pure_deadline_page_profiles_exclude_verification_teardown` exercises the four surviving-key profiles with verification disabled: the target remains alive after source removal. FRAME places a Result-returning benchmark's final expression outside the verification guard, so these profiles keep their verification helper as a separate statement and return only `Ok(())`. The complete downward cost envelope and maximal legal encoded authority records remain unqualified; these extra profiles add no production `WeightInfo` binding or complete close bound.

The production `benchmarking.rs::close_actor` retains a maximum-Step Mutable User Manual Contract and real Suspended Block retry, with maximum signed funding width, a present window and maximum legal balance-watch configuration. Balance activation excludes an auto-close nonce; the stored header-width assertion accounts for this and the Manual Trigger. Configuration does not fabricate Parked residence: no dependency plan/episode or temporal Trigger exists while the Run is Suspended. `production_retry_close_reaches_full_manual_header` and the exact benchmark-Wasm profile verify reachability, custody and owner-hold release. Retained Parked/Suspended both-deep diagnostics cover independent clock repairs, not a complete union bound; watch count and per-source page occupancy remain separate cost inputs.

The Parked both-deep fixture adds 31 real System peers with the target's balance watches. Manual readiness and ordinary `StopCycle` completion park them; negative reviews move earlier peer deadlines beyond the target without rewriting handles. Every target registration page has 32 occupied slots and its maximum encoded width before close. Exact page/header/free-position checks prove only the target registration is removed; peer authority snapshots and native/watched-asset balances remain unchanged, while the target's hold and both clock keys are reclaimed. `signed_parked_two_clock_close_releases_both_indices_and_keeps_custody` reaches the fixture with pre/post try-state; a freshly built benchmark-Wasm exact-profile run also passes. Its low-repeat output separates recorded proof bytes, generated proof estimate and database counts. Neither result certifies the complete cleanup maximum or production-Wasm execution.

`tests/lifecycle.rs::user_close_reclaims_park_and_pending_without_custody_or_hold_leak` checks custody/hold cleanup; `canonical_owner_close_releases_process_and_deadline_residence` checks retry removal. `tests/scheduling.rs::canonical_terminal_removal_closes_service_authority_transactionally` rejects a late finalizer failure with exact storage-root rollback. These functional falsifiers do not certify RefTime, ProofSize or database maxima. Complete owner coverage and generated rebinding remain open in `Actors/Service Failure And Terminal` in the root backlog.

## Actors Read-Model Contract

This subsystem follows the project-wide [`read-model.contract.en.md`](../../../../docs/deos/read-model.contract.en.md) split.

### Canonical on-chain Actor projections

The current pallet provides chain-native bounded reads for known Actor and scheduler truth through:

- `actor_semantic_states(actor_id)`, `actor_hot(actor_id)`, `actor_contract(actor_id)`, and `active_actor_state(actor_id)` for the current lifecycle, Contract, generation, admission, and optional Run projection
- `actor_processes(actor_id)`, `service_header()`, `service_nodes(actor_id)`, and exact Deadline handles/pages for current process residence and bounded physical authority
- `actor_state_hold(actor_id)` for the retained User hold authority; System Actors remain hold-exempt
- `owner_slot_bitmap(owner)` plus deterministic `sovereign_account_id(owner, owner_slot)` and `sovereign_index(sovereign)` for bounded per-owner recovery
- Deterministic `sovereign_account_id_system(sovereign_id)` for System Actor addressing against the known runtime catalog
- `ActorEligibilityApi` and `ActorCostApi` for bounded semantic eligibility and separated current cost projections
- `ActiveActorLimit`, `GlobalCircuitBreaker`, `IdleStarvationState`, bounded current faults, operational events, and live execution side effects

`SovereignAccountDeriver` maps User custody from `SCALE(ActorsPalletId, b"user", owner, owner_slot)` and System custody from `SCALE(ActorsPalletId, b"system", sovereign_id)`. The host owns the concrete infallible `AccountId` mapping; explicit tags separate the two deterministic account domains before hashing, and the DEOS runtime preserves its established 32-byte identities.

These are authoritative bounded surfaces for known-Actor inspection, per-owner recovery, scheduler state, and current operator observability. Funding policy is part of the Contract; Actors exposes no funding accumulator or historical funding projection.

Full Active classification validates semantic generation, process status/residence, Service or Deadline authority, Contract/admission identity, optional Run, current Step, and independent Trigger deadline without scanning the Actor population. TryRuntime adds Contract-tail, detector, hold and reverse-ownership checks. Its carrier audits may scan retained topology; they are read-only and compiled only with `try-runtime`, not part of scheduler admission or execution.

| Audit owner | Carrier invariants |
| --- | --- |
| `do_try_state_service_ring` | Exact Active/process/node generations and kinds; count/cursor agreement; one complete cyclic ring with reciprocal links and no disconnected members |
| `do_try_state_deadline_carrier` | Exact map-key/semantic owners for both handle families; distinct occupied slots; C32 page/header/count and vacancy-set agreement; packed clock heaps with exact reverse positions and no orphan pages |

The `try_state_carrier_*` regressions corrupt otherwise valid admitted state and prove rejection without root mutation. They cover ownerless records, stale references, split rings, slot aliasing, detached owned pages, vacancy cycles and index drift; specific errors distinguish detached-page and heap-packing checks from earlier failures. Lawful witnesses retain empty round markers, noncontiguous page ids, process Deadline handles without hot pointers, and independent temporal handles beside Disabled, Service, retry or Parked residence. These audits do not establish production Weight containment.

### Indexed / materialized Actor views

The pallet intentionally does **not** promise these as canonical on-chain surfaces:

- Long-lived per-actor execution history
- Per-step timeline replay across many cycles
- Fleet-wide dashboards, rankings, and operator analytics across arbitrary actor sets
- Archived run logs or forensic traces beyond bounded recent on-chain observability

Those belong to events plus external indexing/materialization rather than permanent in-kernel storage.

### Current boundary for actor discovery

Actor discovery is intentionally split by use case:

- User-facing recovery/discovery is chain-native only within the bounded owner-slot space: read `owner_slot_bitmap(owner)`, derive occupied sovereign accounts, and resolve them through `sovereign_index`
- System Actor discovery is chain-native for the known runtime catalog because `actor_id` values and sovereign derivation are deterministic
- Arbitrary fleet-wide discovery across all actors is still an indexed/materialized view unless a future bounded runtime projection is added

## Extrinsics (Implementation Surface)

| Call | Extrinsic | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `0` | `create_user_actor` | fee; complete Active or Dormant contract input; no User `Mint` |
| `1` | `create_user_actor_at_slot` | exact slot; same complete Active or Dormant input |
| `2` | `create_system_actor` | governance origin; explicit mutability and complete Active or Dormant contract input |
| `3` | `create_system_actor_at_sovereign_id` | attach a fresh System identity to an allocated vacant custody locator with a complete Active or Dormant contract input |
| `4` | `pause_actor` | Mutable System Actors only |
| `5` | `resume_actor` | Mutable System Actors only |
| `6` | `manual_trigger` | latch readiness and publish Service/Parked destination |
| `7` | removed | retired separate funding mutation; `update_contract` owns authored replacement |
| `8` | `close_actor` | prechecked pure destruction in place |
| `9` | `update_contract` | mutable actors; atomically replace the complete authored Actor Contract |
| `10` | `set_global_circuit_breaker` | breaker control |
| `11` | `permissionless_sweep` | liveness touchpoint, no normal cycle |
| `12` | removed | retired separate steps/completion mutation; `update_contract` owns authored replacement |
| `13` | `set_active_actor_limit` | governance operational cap tuning |
| `14` | `permissionless_sweep_many` | bounded batch touchpoint, no process publication |
| `15..=16` | reserved | retired field-specific auto-close mutations; `update_contract` owns authored replacement |
| `17` | removed | retired close-plan mutation |
| `18..=20` | reserved | retired transitional dormant creation calls; canonical User/System creation expresses dormancy as absent Contract |
| `21` | `activate_actor` | typed Active Actor Contract with schedule, `ContractSteps`, funding policy, and admission validation |
| `22` | `deactivate_actor` | remove contract/scheduler state while preserving identity and balances |

Calls `4`, `5`, `6`, `8`, `9`, `21`, and `22` use the class-specific control authority: signed owner for User actors, signed owner or governance for System actors. Active-only calls reject dormant identities; `close_actor` handles either lifecycle.

## Validation Coverage

Package validation lives in the `src/tests.rs` fixture/module root, domain suites under `src/tests/`, `src/benchmarking.rs`, the independent `embedding-runtime`, and compile-time exhaustive semantic contracts. Tests pin SCALE indices, storage names and types, Actor-state decomposition, scheduler/Trigger/lifecycle invariants, Task atomicity, retry transitions, custody conservation, and try-state reconciliation.

The current no-policy, Close-funded ordinary Service path authenticates stored certificate self-hash/body/Run/generation/residence bindings without reconstructing the host's current admission authority. These are different checks: `ActorAdmissionCertificate::has_valid_identity` is not `admission_matches_current_authority`. An explicit native host-semantics change before a latched Idle Actor's first Opening still commits a Transfer for both classes. That is an implementation gap against specification §4.4, not a permitted fast path or a cache invariant. The probe changes the Test authority without recertification; it is not healthy production history or a complete runtime-upgrade simulation.

Additional native host-fault probes show stale zero-Step Cycle completion, stale next-Step execution from a genuinely paid Run, and a committed Transfer after authority changes inside Pipeline fee collection. The insolvency control already refuses through the existing terminal path and preserves its root; stale apoptosis is not a confirmed defect. For these unqualified host transitions, §4.4 refusal and §14.2 retention must compose: blocking work does not cancel, refund, recertify or reopen an already-paid Run. An evidenced deployment migration remains a separate owner.

The source-qualified correction seams are paid canonical classification and post-Opening callback validation, not a global low-level-loader change. Complete state-probe, System/User Opening, zero-Step and refusal prices remain prerequisites; intended specification semantics and production code are unchanged.

A temporary native candidate exercised those actual classification/Opening seams, not a copied scheduler. Ten tests across 22 contexts per mode cover the prior fault probes, valid zero-/one-/two-Step cycles, both classification shortage dimensions and known-zero-effect post-fee rollback with a committed prefix. Its live all-feature counts for these no-policy controls are two at Opening and one on a later Running Step; refusing before classification admission performs no query. Removing explicit known-zero Task evidence fails the meter assertion, demonstrating that root equality alone is insufficient. The candidate was removed byte-exactly because physical owners remain unqualified. This is finite functional evidence, not retained implementation or a claim that stale Service is repaired.

`benchmarking.rs::scheduler_actor_state_probe` stages the complete cached current-Step load/current-authority comparison as one recorded composition. Existing native-only observations reset after setup, expect one provider call in either SDK verification mode and detect its omission. Root/fixture verification stays outside recording; diagnostic counter code is absent from benchmark Wasm. The retained source is the existing System/Manual Running fixture, not all legal classes, headers, fragments, failures or host providers. Changing this benchmark neither adds a production Service guard nor rebinds a Weight coefficient or historical artifact.

The existing `scheduler_actor_state_probe_wide_header` keeps `0..3` as loader-only categories and stages new full-span `4..7` User/System Running/Suspended sources plus `8/9` User/System Running sources with a full later current fragment. Existing public-prefix constructors and canonical retry/Q1 retain authored history, balance snapshot and legal Parked-without-nonce headers. New spans authenticate the returned admission once; native SDK verification on/off checks class/phase/cursor/width and zero/one current-provider calls. The later constructor appends peers, so its source is the canonical frontier, not the last allocated id. These are finite source domains, not complete host/failure or legal geometry maxima; old timing evidence remains source-bound.

The test/runtime-benchmarks-only `service_head_allowance_diagnostic` returns a conservative envelope for the current eligible nonempty no-policy/no-wait head using existing declared owners. It reads current-fragment geometry without materializing the repeated-Step Contract view, includes possible Close and outer placement, and maps StopCycle effect work to Control. Unsupported or locally incoherent heads return None. Process generation/status/residence/attempt are checked before canonical semantic loading, so an unsupported waiting process cannot enter optional financial authentication. The same fresh process is passed directly to the existing loader without a callback boundary.

`src/tests/service_head_allowance.rs` checks committed root/header and fee-call neutrality, canonical paired-domain fit, current-tail isolation, rollback, stale bindings and reciprocal peers. Its SDK-recorder witness commits setup first, records only an unsupported-process query, positively observes the process key and excludes financial, policy, head, Run and current-tail reads. Root snapshots stay outside recording. This is not an execution permit, reservation or production API; its own inspection cost is unpriced and excluded. Native model fit/read-key isolation does not qualify physical maxima or a production callback wrapper.

The same test owner contains a single-unit `conditional_quantum` model, excluded from production and runtime benchmarks. Its parent prepays entry/refusal while retaining the existing head-independent Service floor and finalization. Each query is reserved before storage access; the complete existing two-clock worker and second guard are admitted together with outer work and the head. It uses the shared `BlockResourceState` and real transactional worker, never a copied scheduler or cached Actor authority. Post-query fit uses the updated Rust ledger, so storage rollback does not refund incurred Control. Ambiguous future/invalid-destination errors conservatively reject the unit rather than adding a classifier or repair.

Five conditional-unit tests cover twelve isolated contexts: paired-axis bootstrap boundaries, paired effect refusal, next-block readiness, nonproductive rollback after earlier committed units, actual collector callback invalidation and fee failure. The one-shot mock collector hook runs after a successful ledger transfer and is cleared between fixtures. A callback closes the Mutable protected head; the worker succeeds, but fresh head refusal rolls back only this unit's close/readiness/fee. Ordinary Service remains available afterward. Nonstorage fee-call observations intentionally survive rollback and are not committed debits.

Nonzero entry/guard/outer token prices are explicit assumptions, not physical bounds or production coefficients. The tests cover isolated Prepass-phase composition with an earlier real clock pair, not complete Executive chronology, all callback/branch domains, conservative guard/transaction pricing or capacity. They introduce no production caller, benchmark sample, fee, storage or ABI change.

Replayable state-machine traces cover suspension, continuation, cancellation, exclusive process residence, owner slots, and current balances. The seeded model drives create/activate/deactivate/signal/trigger/pause/resume/Contract-update/Service/Deadline/execute/close/slot-round-trip/suspend/continue/cancel sequences against conservation, cross-store invariants, and try-state after every operation. Temporary DEX failure and recovery exercise randomized fault suspension and repair. Each control operation admits only its typed lifecycle event family, including ordered cancellation and summary events when replacing, deactivating, or closing a suspended cycle.

The test-only [`behavior_reference`](../src/tests/behavior_reference.rs) oracle replays committed outcome traces and derives exact totals/streaks from histories independently of the production sparse/capped monitor. It distinguishes normal completion (including early `StopCycle`) from clean plan-end completion, checks episode resets and closure after the current Step, and guards the cross-Cycle streak counterexample and the two-Step conditional-transfer form. Its traces supply continuation dispositions; it does not implement Task adapters, retry-authority classification, fees, financial deadlines, scheduler admission or storage. Passing these tests establishes reference semantics only, not production policy integration or runtime differential conformance.

`behavior_runtime_seeded_native_traces_match_independent_oracle_and_payment` generates bounded native Transfer/conditional StopCycle plans and outcome decisions under one fixed Proptest seed, replaying each case for User and System. Prepared policy geometry then uses canonical Service: all ten capped metrics are compared with independent exact histories and authored caps, while native transfers, separate Trigger/Pipeline/Action payments, fee exemption, cursor/nonce and policy Close are checked together. Selected direct Step attempts force late control refusal and preserve the complete FRAME root before ordinary Service retries them. AND/OR atoms mature independently across normal completion, return, skip, failure and abort. The model keeps its independent global-failure terminal beyond the finite trace. Public policy admission, reference adapters, waiting, replacement and physical prices retain separate qualification gates.

`behavior_runtime_scalar_terminals_match_oracle_in_both_tracking_modes` closes each of the ten individual metrics at thresholds one and two for both classes. Native Transfer and conditional StopCycle traces include opposite outcomes, absent observations, skipped economic Steps, early return and failed-then-clean plan-end completion. The oracle selects the exact terminal attempt; late control refusal preserves the whole FRAME root before canonical Service commits that attempt, its permitted effects/payment and one policy Close. Current-outcome rules have no historical counter, while first-clean tracking retains only its required Cycle flags; accumulated projections match authored caps. Committed recipient credits survive every later refusal and Close. These prepared sources retain the same public-admission, reference-adapter, wait/replacement and physical qualification boundaries.

`behavior_runtime_accumulated_count_commits_only_with_its_action_and_close` extends the terminal invariant across User and System. A real mock-ledger Transfer first commits one success. Late control refusal for either class and Action-collector refusal for User restore the complete FRAME root, monitor, holds, recipient credit and fee balances; incurred effect Weight remains available. The independent committed-history oracle rejects the rolled-back result. Repair commits the second success and one policy Close, with one Action fee only for User without a second Pipeline fee or normal-completion observation. The scalar-count branch keeps three Steps and closes before plan end. Rollback-only simulation agrees on Opening and terminal status, cursor and accumulated results, and preserves the full root on success or typed refusal. Public policy admission, reference adapters, physical maxima and tariffs remain unqualified.

The same transaction witness also covers `ActionSuccessTotal >= 1 AND CycleCompletedTotal >= 1` over two Transfers for both classes, with User collector refusal and both-class actual-control refusal. The first success alone leaves the Actor live; final plan-end completion and the permitted Action select one atomic Close. Repair pays only the remaining User Action fee, preserving the first transfer and original Pipeline payment. The independent oracle distinguishes plan-end closure from scalar-count closure before plan end. This is prepared native model conformance, not public creation/certificate admission or reference Weight qualification.

The shared `behavior_runtime` source fixture creates a Dormant identity, composes a current model certificate covering configured policy cleanup, and calls `insert_active_actor_with_admission` inside the lifecycle transaction before hold reconciliation and public Manual readiness. It no longer swaps policy/certificate records into an already-triggered no-policy Actor. The test-only lifecycle model keeps financial resource ports disabled and semantics-version changes observable, so stale-authority/re-certification tests retain their force. This exercises the internal installation-to-service path, not public policy admission or production coefficients.

`financial_wait_runtime_certified_hint_returns_each_boundary_and_commits_only_owed_work` also checks rollback-only simulation for AtTime, Opening, Running and Suspended waits. Before a hint and at its tentative B+1 Service return, simulation reports `NotReady`, preserves the complete root and collects no owed fee. Actual Service then commits the original boundary, retaining the episode, absolute expiry and paid progress until that commit. The combined behavior, financial-wait and simulation selection passes 116 default and 115 benchmark/try-runtime checks with no ignored tests. Prepared wait sources remain separate from public admission, reference adapters, economic backing and physical qualification.

`financial_wait_runtime_public_sweep_preserves_wait_and_independent_window_terminal` compares single and batched public sweep over prepared wait sources with rollback-only simulation. AtTime and unexpired Opening/Running/Suspended remain waiting. An expired Window on Opening/Running/Suspended refuses a zero Control budget, then projects the shared custody-neutral Close with sufficient synthetic resources. Running and Suspended retain their paid prefix, nonce, cursor and accumulated result; the projection has no new Step. Simulation preserves the whole root and local custody. Actual sweep preserves all earlier events, fees and native/local balances, emits no new Action/recovery receipt, and removes the wait, Run and Deadline. This is native model conformance, not public waiting admission or a physical Close bound.

`behavior_runtime_reconfiguration_preserves_or_resets_committed_episode` covers Idle, Running and Suspended for both classes, each with technical re-certification and semantic replacement. A native Transfer commits the prefix; Suspended reaches a real second temporary AddLiquidity refusal and Deadline. Exact no-op preserves the root even under newer host authority; stale execution loading refuses. Replacement cancels the Run/retry once without completion credit. Rollback restores the full root, holds and custody. Compatible re-certification preserves the episode; replacement resets it. Canonical Service reaches the oracle-selected Close with prior credits retained and old generation/source rejected. The actual public `update_contract` policy-removal caller also succeeds inside forced rollback. AddLiquidity is a logical mock outcome, not reference liquidity economics. Public policy admission, reference adapters, waiting and physical binding retain separate gates.

The same reconfiguration witness rejects a current host certificate one unit short of full policy Close in either Weight dimension across User/System, Idle/Running/Suspended and replacement/re-certification. It also rejects self-consistent certificate hashes with foreign wake qualification, a non-current host version or an ungranted inflated lifecycle bound. The store boundary preserves its own pre-call root before rollback; the enclosing transaction restores prior cancellation, Run/retry residence, monitor, holds and committed credits. A valid certificate retains the existing commit/rollback and episode-preservation/reset assertions. The check adds policy validation work to the caller; it does not qualify public dispatch Weight, Creation backing or a newly enabled admission domain.

Mandatory falsifiers cover Service/Deadline saturation, protected User fee-native floor, collector failure after admission, invalid `Fresh`, and nonce exhaustion for both classes, all ending in try-state.

The raw extra `service_head_allowance(c)` measures only the diagnostic. Original cases 0–23 retain six class/phase sources and three labelled invariant faults per source; two public Manual peers supply distinct reciprocal links without moving the target cursor. Cases 24/25 add User/System Running with a complete second tail: the shared prefix constructor authors ContinueNextStep at Steps 2–4, then ordinary Q1 visits advance its paid Run to cursor 5. These profiles require at least nine admitted Steps. No cursor, process or generation is rewritten, and existing sources keep their meanings. Construction, source faults, root snapshots and verification remain outside the measured body.

Native verification on/off covers 26 profiles (52 contexts). The geometry test separately compares original and later sources for both classes: Test chunks are 816 bytes with Task widths `[31,65,65,65]` versus 854 bytes with `[65,65,65,65]`; the retained Running snapshot is present. Removing noncurrent tails under rollback leaves the query result unchanged and restores the root; that deliberate isolation fault is not publication history. These are codec observations, not trie proof or a universal fragment maximum. Maximum-prefix Run already includes its balance snapshot, and a Parked header cannot legally add nonce auto-close; neither is an uncovered field that can simply be appended. Complete guard/entry/refusal/outer-transaction pricing remains unqualified.

FRAME benchmarks isolate bounded package branches; every production host must prove fixture reachability and envelope coverage before generating and binding runtime-specific weights. A Fixed-Transfer Suspended-head assurance matrix uses authored maximum-length Contracts, real sovereign custody, Opening, due return, and a second retry across host-feasible current-predicate counts. It checks current-state reevaluation, custody, and canonical reconciliation; it does not establish a Weight envelope for other head Tasks or all host configurations.

Under both `std` and `runtime-benchmarks`, `Pallet::prepare_parked_handoff_close_witness` returns an Actor id plus opaque Close and verification closures. It reuses the existing parked handoff constructor; fixture/check types remain private. The caller owns externalities, setup commit, recorder, ordinary root and transaction outcome. Close invokes the public signed-origin Pallet call; verification checks complete destruction and retained source authority afterward, never during the body/root snapshot. Preparation errors propagate and existing invariant checks remain enforced. This optional native diagnostic is not a consensus hook, selector, price API, Executive signature check or complete physical bound.

External-consumer profiles prove that the crate composes without DEOS types. Concrete runtime adapters, generated artifacts, stress SLOs, and operational gates belong to the integration architecture.

The AddLiquidity effect benchmark prepares a public User identity with the matching authored Step and measures `execution.rs::benchmark_prepared_add_liquidity`, which invokes the ordinary Task executor including outcome checks, nested transaction and receipt. The raw-only `task_add_liquidity_user_refusal` profile uses an optional benchmark host fixture for real auxiliary native deposits and verifies whole-root rollback on permanent funding refusal; unsupported hosts explicitly skip. The reference integration owns concrete asset/deposit geometry, fresh-Wasm artifacts and raw metrics. Neither profile measures surrounding Step control/fee settlement or establishes complete physical maxima.

The three raw-only System extras cover anchored pool creation, anchor-capacity refusal and an unanchored existing pool. They use the same executor with explicit System class, public identity creation/activation and optional host fixtures returning LP identity and expected output. Success verifies exact input debits and the LP receipt; refusal restores the complete root. Unsupported hosts skip these fixtures. The reference integration owns their qualification and concrete geometry; no new production charge owner is introduced.

Stake and Unstake likewise prepare public User identities and matching authored Steps, then invoke ordinary Task execution through their benchmark-only helpers; verification authenticates the exact invocation receipt. Their raw-only consumer-refusal extras restore the whole root on permanent funding refusal. Optional host fixtures default to unsupported and explicitly skip, preserving generic embedding; reference first-consumer geometry, fresh-Wasm samples and diagnostics belong to the integration owner. Task-local coverage does not establish surrounding fees/control or complete maxima.

The DEX main profiles measure complete prepared System Task execution and verify exact factual swap receipts. Raw-only User success extras create public identities with matching authored Steps and exercise the User capacity guard. Optional first-nonsufficient-output extras retain complete Native input funding, then verify permanent funding refusal and whole-root rollback. Unsupported hosts explicitly skip those refusal fixtures. The reference integration owns concrete pool/provider geometry and source-bound Wasm samples; Task-local coverage excludes surrounding Step control and fee settlement and does not certify complete class/account/failure maxima.

RemoveLiquidity now likewise prepares a public User with the matching authored Step and invokes its ordinary Task executor, including the LP reverse-binding check, host transaction/capacity guard, output validation and exact factual receipt. Its raw-only refusal extra uses an optional host fixture for absent nonsufficient outputs with third-party-deposited LP custody and verifies whole-root rollback. Unsupported hosts explicitly skip. Concrete reference account/pool geometry and applicable Wasm evidence belong to the integration owner; surrounding Step control/fees and complete maxima retain separate qualification.

Transfer, Burn and DonateLiquidity now create public User identities with matching authored Steps and invoke the ordinary prepared Task executor. Transfer retains its certified Actor-recipient ingress fixture and verifies both custody deltas plus the exact receipt; Burn verifies actual debit and its receipt. Donation fixtures declare input cap and expected donated A/B outputs, so receipt verification does not confuse newly acquired B receipts with pre-existing B debit. The independently embedded helper follows the same benchmark-only tuple contract and keeps unsupported capability explicit. Concrete reference staking/pool geometry and current Wasm samples belong to the integration owner; these profiles exclude surrounding Step control/fee settlement and do not certify complete maxima.

The default `scheduler_inner_opening_user_progress_min(t)` reuses `UserPaged` but records Action-liability calculation, carried-plan construction and the production inner Service Step, not the full FIFO wrapper. Source discovery, fixture checks and hold/balance snapshots stay outside; production revalidations remain inside. Zero-resolution progress isolates the User Pipeline payment/receipt and hold path without Task effects or Action fees. Verification requires nonzero exact Pipeline collection, unchanged hold and retained Running cursor one. `WeightInfo` requires this separate User owner; a class-agnostic host context must cover both User and System progress, with Action collection/receipt independently owned. Full FIFO or wrapper measurements are not interchangeable with this boundary. Concrete coefficient binding and artifact qualification belong to the host integration owner.

The `action_invocation_receipt` benchmark measures a bounded invoked-receipt envelope: optional maximum-width Temporary `StepFailed` plus maximum-width `ActionFeeCharged`, asserting both events. The failed-result event is emitted by the Step core after the prepared Task returns, not by the prepared-effect owner. The fee receipt remains after publication/settlement; the benchmark does not relocate either production event or price Task execution, fee transfer or lifecycle work. Host integration owns adopted coefficients and applicability; whitelisted event storage is not zero physical storage cost.

The generated `scheduler_inner_suspended_user_tail_retry(s,p)` parameterizes the existing retry constructor by User class and retains the old System wrapper. Independent native funding and non-fee-native predicates isolate a zero-effect retry with unchanged User hold and no Action debit. `scheduler_inner_suspended_user_tail_progress(s,p)` uses the optional temporary-Transfer fixture to produce a paid recipient refusal, then exhausts input outside recording so the measured retry skips resolution and advances without effects/fees. Both timers call the production inner Service path with liability/plan work included and discovery/fixture checks excluded. Their native endpoint tests preserve backoff, cursor/reset, Service, hold and custody outcomes. Both are required `WeightInfo` owners included in default generation; host integration owns qualified coefficients, User/System selector unions and artifact binding. The generated models are not universal host/currency/trie maxima.

The extra `task_transfer_minimum(a)` diagnostic uses the host's optional temporary-Transfer fixture, requiring a non-fee-native asset and a positive amount below its deposit minimum. Setup leaves source custody at `minimum + amount`; `a0` keeps the recipient empty and `a1` funds it to exactly the minimum. Only the ordinary prepared Transfer executor is recorded. Verification checks Temporary `RecipientDepositUnavailable` with unchanged custody/events, or exact successful debits/credits and the factual receipt. The explicit native benchmark test exercises both categorical endpoints; unsupported host setup fails explicitly. This diagnostic supplies no generated owner, surrounding Step/fee price or complete physical bound; runtime asset geometry and collection belong to integration documentation.

The Mint effect profile now creates a public System AddressEvent Actor with an authored Burn prefix and Mint successor. Certified source-less funding latches Idle readiness; ordinary Service executes the Burn and publishes the real Running Run. The measured prepared Mint uses that Run nonce/cursor, credits its own sovereign and verifies the exact receipt, unchanged progress/Service/identity, zero Fee Sink change and no pending future Cycle. Hosts unable to admit two Steps explicitly skip. Task-local verification preserves the Run rather than simulating Step completion; the reference integration owns separate ordinary-Service completion/next-block conformance, artifact identity and sample scope. No complete physical maximum follows from this selected no-policy Native fixture.

## Integration Handoff

A production host must bind generated `WeightInfo`, concrete adapters and origins, defensible Service/Deadline/Actor bounds, fee conversion and collection, ingress producers, genesis Actors, and independent runtime evidence. The package embedding guide owns that checklist; [`docs/deos/actors.integration.en.md`](../../../../docs/deos/actors.integration.en.md) records the DEOS realization.

Implementation mirror for [Specification](./specification.en.md).

Prepared `ActorEligibilityApi` v8 carries the host Balance type through its Active result. A retained financial episode projects its original unpaid service/liability and unchanged entry/expiry alongside current fee-native spendability from `AssetOps::balance` above `MinUserBalance` and the exact non-negative shortfall. The companion and process authenticate the original boundary; current residence remains independent, including tentative funded Service. The query performs no fee, Action, receipt publication or state mutation, and zero shortfall does not certify execution admission or collector success. Non-wait projections perform no additional currency lookup. Native financial fixtures cover every unpaid source, protected-minimum/debt boundaries, large balances and preserved roots; the integration owner binds checks and isolated native metadata. Storage geometry, scheduler transitions and tariffs are unchanged; production API/metadata/Wasm binding remains gated.
